plan-2026-09-17T064004-0d194df2/D-003 | 2026-09-17 | Removed --show-plots' show=True forwarding entirely (all visualize() calls pass show=False unconditionally) rather than trying to make the flag "work" under the forced Agg backend; Step 7 dropped the flag from the parser via the existing _drop_base_parser_args() helper, consistent with the prior plan's D-005/D-006 disposal mechanism for dead CLI knobs.
plan-2026-09-17T064004-0d194df2/D-004 | 2026-09-17 | Pointed create_visualization_manager() at output_dir=run_dir/"visualizations" with both experiment_name="" and timestamp="" to land PNGs at run_dir/visualizations/<name>.png with no double nesting and no second independent timestamp, superseding the prior plan's D-002 whose double-nesting hazard no longer has the same shape under this plan's unified run_dir layout.
plan-2026-09-17T064004-0d194df2/D-005 | 2026-09-17 | Added an optional filename_suffix parameter to the shared render_function_and_spline_grid() helper so KANVisualizationCallback's periodic grid renders get epoch-stamped filenames (mirroring bfunet's per-epoch snapshot-history convention) while plot_results()'s final post-hoc call keeps the plain, canonical function_approximation.png/kan_splines.png names.
plan-2026-09-17T064004-0d194df2/D-006 | 2026-09-17 | Reordered model.fit()'s callbacks=[...] list so ModelCheckpoint/CSVLogger precede KANGridUpdateCallback, fixing a CRITICAL bug (found by adversarial review, confirmed via same-seed A/B weight comparison) where the grid-update callback's in-place weight mutation ran before checkpoint save/selection, silently corrupting best_model.keras on any grid-update epoch.
plan-2026-09-17T052443-2c932602/D-002 | 2026-09-17 | Fixed train_kan.py's output-dir resolution by inlining the same repo-root-anchoring pattern already used by train.common.args (parents[3]) rather than extracting a brand-new shared helper, since only one confirmed call site exists.
plan-2026-09-17T052443-2c932602/D-004 | 2026-09-17 | Closed the "trained model never persisted" gap with a minimal model.save() to REPO_ROOT/results/kan_regression/kan_model.keras, rejecting create_callbacks() because its Pattern-1-classification defaults (val_accuracy monitor, EpochAnalyzerCallback) do not fit this synthetic-regression smoke script.
plan-2026-09-17T052443-2c932602/D-005 | 2026-09-17 | Added a local _drop_base_parser_args() helper to remove the dead --dataset/--patience (later extended to --image-size/--weight-decay/--lr-schedule) CLI actions from train_kan.py's own parser instance, rather than deleting them from the shared create_base_argument_parser() and breaking ~10 other trainers that use them live.
plan-2026-09-17T052443-2c932602/D-006 | 2026-09-17 | Replaced train_kan.py's "if parsed value == old default" CLI-override footgun with parser.set_defaults(...) called before parse_args(), so an explicit CLI value equal to the base parser's stock default is no longer silently clobbered back to the KAN-preferred default.
plan-2026-09-17T111913-932b1348/D-001 | 2026-09-17 | Added a `depthwise: bool = False` constructor/get_config field to GaborFiltersInitializer, letting create_gabor_depthwise_conv2d opt into the correct kh*kw depthwise fan-in formula instead of the cross-channel kh*kw*in_ch one, while create_gabor_conv2d keeps the False default and stays byte-identical to its pre-fix behavior.
plan-2026-09-17T132602-7a6ebdb4/D-006 | 2026-09-17 | Added `init_scheme`/`init_seed` constructor params to KAN, auto-injecting the already-implemented but previously-unused create_kan_initializers (Rigas et al.) per layer to break the base_scaler='ones' fan-in-uncompensated symmetry that both caused a real training run's val_loss plateau and the pre-existing GF-01/R-121 dead-gradient-at-init finding, with init_scheme=None preserving the old bare KANLinear behavior exactly and KANLinear itself left untouched.
plan-2026-09-17T194331-3ce35186/D-006 | 2026-09-18 | Replicated the model.py init_scheme/init_seed injection pattern at the FFN factory's 'kan' type: per-key injection checked against the raw `kwargs` dict (not the merged `final_params`), popped before construction, validated against VALID_KAN_SCHEMES.
plan-2026-09-17T194331-3ce35186/D-007 | 2026-09-18 | Added a `FACTORY_SYNTHETIC_PARAMS` exemption table to test_factory_registry_drift.py's PLACEMENT check, since `init_scheme`/`init_seed` are factory-only dispatch keys popped before `KANLinear(...)` construction and were never meant to reach its named signature.
plan-2026-09-17T194331-3ce35186/D-008 | 2026-09-18 | Added `kan_init_scheme`/`kan_init_seed` opt-in to SingleWindowAttention's kan_key mode via an unconditional both-key `**kan_key_kwargs` splat (empty at default, never both-keys-as-None) to exactly reproduce KANLinear's bare defaults, with `grid_range` resolved via live `inspect.signature` introspection rather than a hardcoded literal.
plan-2026-09-17T194331-3ce35186/D-009 | 2026-09-18 | Logs a loud `logger.warning` inside `create_ffn_layer`'s 'kan' branch whenever `init_scheme`'s per-key injection is silently shadowed by a caller/wrapper-forwarded kwarg (e.g. via `assemble_ffn_config`), rather than redesigning the 22-FFN-type-shared wrapper helper for one type's opt-in knob with zero live callers today.
plan-2026-09-17T194331-3ce35186/D-010 | 2026-09-18 | Added `kan_init_scheme`/`kan_init_seed` to the 4 window-family `ATTENTION_REGISTRY` entries' `optional_params` so the Site-2 knob is reachable through `create_attention_layer`, a pure pass-through requiring zero new dispatch logic.
plan-2026-09-18T045308-c89cdf76/D-002 | 2026-09-18 | The trainer calls model.train_hebbian(x_train, y_train, epochs=1, ...) once per outer-loop iteration rather than once with epochs=N, so per-epoch checkpoint/CSV/visualization cadence stays reachable.
plan-2026-09-18T045308-c89cdf76/D-003 | 2026-09-18 | Built a fresh, purpose-built argparse.ArgumentParser for train_mothnet.py rather than reusing create_base_argument_parser() plus pruning, since MothNet shares almost nothing with the base parser's surface.
plan-2026-09-18T045308-c89cdf76/D-004 | 2026-09-18 | Hand-rolled two pure functions (render_training_dashboard, render_mb_sparsity) rather than registering VisualizationManager plugins, since the earned-abstraction rule needs >=2 concrete call sites and MothNet has only one.
plan-2026-09-18T045308-c89cdf76/D-005 | 2026-09-18 | Within each epoch, train_hebbian's single mutation is called strictly before val_accuracy/train_accuracy are measured, which is strictly before the checkpoint-save/CSV-row/viz-history decision, with a code comment documenting the ordering.
plan-2026-09-18T045308-c89cdf76/D-009 | 2026-09-18 | build_model() calls model.build((None, input_dim)) explicitly before the training loop starts, so best_model.keras/final_model.keras are always savable even under a degenerate --epochs value or an early failure.
plan-2026-09-18T045308-c89cdf76/D-011 | 2026-09-18 | Wired setup_gpu(gpu_id=args.gpu) immediately after parse_arguments returns, importing setup_gpu from train.common alongside the other shared primitives, restoring repo-wide --gpu convention compliance.
plan-2026-09-18T060057-c1cfc3d3/D-001 | 2026-09-18 | Route build_model()'s seeding through train.common.set_seeds(args.seed), called in the exact same position (strictly before MothNet(...) construction) the old np.random.seed(args.seed) call occupied.
plan-2026-09-18T060057-c1cfc3d3/D-007 | 2026-09-18 | Narrow the D-005 ordering-discipline comment's wording to explicitly separate val_accuracy/train_accuracy (genuine post-update reads) from loss (a within-epoch average over intermediate weight states, not post-update).
plan-2026-09-18T060057-c1cfc3d3/D-008 | 2026-09-18 | Wrap the epoch loop in try/finally so final_model.keras + save_training_history_json are still attempted if train_hebbian/model.save()/extract_features raises mid-loop; a save that itself raises is logged, never silently swallowed.
plan-2026-09-18T060057-c1cfc3d3/D-009 | 2026-09-18 | Raise the CLI --mb-units default from 2000 to 16000 after an 8-seed/arm paired-permutation sweep cleared both pre-registered thresholds (p=0.0086 < 0.05 and mean_diff=0.1633 >= 0.07).
plan-2026-09-18T080513-debe8b11/D-001 | 2026-09-18 | Construct one VisualizationManager instance in train_mothnet.py, register the two EXISTING library plugins (ConfusionMatrixVisualization, ActivationVisualization), and call them for the confusion-matrix and AL/MB activation visualizations, rather than hand-rolling either from scratch.
plan-2026-09-18T080513-debe8b11/D-002 | 2026-09-18 | Add a small, local _predict_in_batches(model, x, batch_size) helper function inside train_mothnet.py, not a change to MothNet.extract_features() itself, to fix the trainer's unbatched-eval OOM risk without changing the model's public API contract.
plan-2026-09-18T080513-debe8b11/D-003 | 2026-09-18 | Add extract_al_features() to model.py, mirroring the existing extract_mb_features() convention, rather than reaching into the already-public model.antennal_lobe sub-layer attribute directly from the trainer.
plan-2026-09-18T080513-debe8b11/D-005 | 2026-09-18 | Add a new, dedicated --eval-batch-size CLI flag (default 5000) rather than reusing --batch-size for eval chunking, decoupling the training mini-batch size from the eval-chunk performance knob.
plan-2026-09-18T080513-debe8b11/D-006 | 2026-09-18 | Replace render_mb_sparsity()'s illegible plt.spy() scatter with a per-class MEAN MB-activation-magnitude heatmap, column-binned to ~200 bins, annotated with each class's sparsity fraction, as a hand-rolled pure function.
plan-2026-09-18T080513-debe8b11/D-007 | 2026-09-18 | Fix the adversarial review's CRITICAL (an illegible activation heatmap reintroducing the exact defect class this plan exists to fix) and all 4 WARNINGs, plus 4 of 5 cheap actionable NOTEs, as same-iteration completion-fixes before recommending CLOSE.
plan-2026-09-18T080513-debe8b11/D-008 | 2026-09-18 | Extract the column-binning logic render_mb_sparsity already used into a shared _bin_activation_columns(matrix, max_bins=200) helper, and bin ONLY the mushroom_body array into a separate heatmap-only ActivationData object, fixing the illegible heatmap panel with the same proven binning strategy.
plan-2026-09-18T080513-debe8b11/D-009 | 2026-09-18 | State the fixed MB sparsity fraction ONCE in the plot title, read directly off model.mb_sparsity (the architectural constant), reduce y-tick labels to just the class digit, and delete the now-unused per-class sparsity_by_class computation entirely.
plan-2026-09-18T080513-debe8b11/D-010 | 2026-09-18 | Default --num-train-samples/--num-val-samples to full MNIST (60000/10000), anchored in-code, accepting a measured >20x per-epoch wall-clock cost (~28.5s vs ~1.3s) as the disclosed price of a from-scratch trainer's natural default.
plan-2026-09-18T080513-debe8b11/D-011 | 2026-09-18 | Run one final, third completion-fix round for a second adversarial review pass's 4 WARNINGs (all instances of the same "code fixed, doc left stale" drift class) plus 2 of 3 NOTEs, then close via direct orchestrator re-verification instead of a third review pass.
plan-2026-09-18T110506-e42a44c7/D-002 | 2026-09-18 | Default the new `--readout-weight-bound` CLI flag ON at a real, measured bound (not `None`), with an explicit `<=0` disable sentinel to reproduce the old unbounded behavior.
plan-2026-09-18T110506-e42a44c7/D-003 | 2026-09-18 | Use a symmetric `ValueRangeConstraint(min_value=-B, max_value=B)` rather than the cited paper's literal `[0, w_max]` rail, since glorot_uniform init draws negative weights the paper's rail would zero on first use.
plan-2026-09-18T110506-e42a44c7/D-008 | 2026-09-18 | Row-scope the readout constraint to only the MB units that fired this batch (`fired` mask + `keras.ops.where`), fixing an adversarial-review-found case where a whole-tensor clip could move a silent unit's row and violate the pinned D-018 invariant.
plan-2026-09-18T110506-e42a44c7/D-009 | 2026-09-18 | Change the row-scoping fired-mask criterion from `keras.ops.greater(pre_synaptic, 0.0)` to `keras.ops.not_equal(pre_synaptic, 0.0)` (matching D-018's own silent-unit predicate exactly) and structurally gate the whole block inside `if self.kernel_constraint is not None:`, fixing two defects a second adversarial review pass found in D-008's own fix.
plan-2026-09-18T154913-1f3c0ce8/D-002 | 2026-09-18 | Tighten ResidualBlock's dropout_rate upper bound to the half-open [0.0, 1.0), since keras.layers.Dropout crashes at rate==1.0 under training=True even though the old closed-range check accepted it.
plan-2026-09-18T154913-1f3c0ce8/D-003 | 2026-09-18 | Tighten GLUFFN's dropout_rate upper bound to the half-open [0.0, 1.0), matching D-002, plus fix an import-style violation (from-keras-import qualified to keras.*).
plan-2026-09-18T154913-1f3c0ce8/D-004 | 2026-09-18 | Tighten GeGLUFFN's dropout_rate upper bound to the half-open [0.0, 1.0), matching D-002/D-003, plus fix the same import-style violation.
plan-2026-09-18T154913-1f3c0ce8/D-005 | 2026-09-18 | Tighten OrthoGLUFFN's dropout_rate upper bound to the half-open [0.0, 1.0) and correct a false docstring claim of a fixed 1e-5 l1 regularization floor (OrthoBlock's own l1/l2 defaults are 0.0, never overridden here).
plan-2026-09-18T154913-1f3c0ce8/D-006 | 2026-09-18 | Tighten SwiGLUFFN's dropout_rate upper bound to the half-open [0.0, 1.0) and fix a mismatched dropout build() shape (harmless today since Dropout.build ignores its shape argument, but a real invariant mismatch given this file's reversed call() order relative to its siblings).
plan-2026-09-18T154913-1f3c0ce8/D-010 | 2026-09-18 | Replace CountingFFN's keras.ops.associative_scan with a sequential keras.ops.scan, fixing two real backend RecursionErrors (sequence length 1, and compiled-model.fit() tracing), verified numerically equivalent to the old parallel scan.
plan-2026-09-18T154913-1f3c0ce8/D-011 | 2026-09-18 | Tighten SwinMLP's dropout_rate upper bound to the half-open [0.0, 1.0); re-verified the prior C-01 activity_regularizer single-charge fix still holds by fresh measurement.
plan-2026-09-18T154913-1f3c0ce8/D-012 | 2026-09-18 | Tighten MixerBlock's dropout_rate upper bound to the half-open [0.0, 1.0) and fix an import-style violation; verified no token/channel-mixing axis-swap defect by an unequal-dimension build and a corrected delta-impulse probe.
plan-2026-09-18T154913-1f3c0ce8/D-013 | 2026-09-18 | Fix tversky_projection.py's _inverse_softplus hard floor, which collapsed distinct contrast-scalar initializer draws to one identical constant in 20-25% of unseeded builds, via an injective exponential continuation (later hardened by D-017 after adversarial review found it still underflowed).
plan-2026-09-18T154913-1f3c0ce8/D-014 | 2026-09-18 | Remove factory.py's stale 'differential' gate_activation registry key (DifferentialFFN's gate-less redesign made it unconditionally crash) and correct the 'counting' scope default to 'causal', fixing all 13 pre-existing test_factory.py failures.
plan-2026-09-18T154913-1f3c0ce8/D-015 | 2026-09-18 | Tighten factory.py's own validate_ffn_config dropout_rate bound to the half-open [0.0, 1.0), the dispatch-layer's own copy of the D-002-class fix.
plan-2026-09-18T154913-1f3c0ce8/D-018 | 2026-09-18 | Remove factory.py's dead 'gate_activation' entry from the activation_params validation list and disclose CountingFFN's jit_compile=False requirement in its class docstring and in D-010's anchor text.
plan-2026-09-18T213948-68dcb72c/D-025 | 2026-09-19 | PowerMLP trainer defaults (lecun_normal initializer, unit input scaling, batch normalization on for MNIST) come from the pre-registered 3-seed x 10-epoch MNIST grid: batch norm beat the best non-BN arm by +0.00243 mean test accuracy against a 0.002 bar, so do not turn it back off on MNIST without a new multi-seed grid.
plan-2026-09-18T213948-68dcb72c/D-027 | 2026-09-19 | Do not redraw the PowerMLP training dashboard after every epoch: that cost 40 percent of a 100-epoch run; the dashboard redraws on a cadence derived from the planned epoch count, always at epoch 1 and at the final state.
plan-2026-09-18T213948-68dcb72c/D-028 | 2026-09-19 | Batch normalization is a per-dataset default (MNIST on, CIFAR-10 off) from two pre-registered 3-seed x 10-epoch grids (CIFAR-10 BN mean 0.4791 vs 0.5102 without, worse in 3 of 3 seeds); do not collapse it to one global flag, since either value is the measured loser on the other dataset.
plan-2026-09-18T213948-68dcb72c/D-029 | 2026-09-19 | The epoch-0 numbers (initial-loss guard and dashboard baseline star) come from one training-mode function, never model.evaluate, which reads the batch-norm model in inference mode (9.7e10 vs 2.68); and a reused --experiment-name is refused (FileExistsError), never merged, overwritten, deleted or auto-suffixed.
plan-2026-09-18T213948-68dcb72c/D-031 | 2026-09-19 | The untrained-metrics training-mode pass restores every dropout SeedGenerator state as well as the weights, so a diagnostic does not shift the dropout stream of the run it measures (same seed gave 0.9836 before and 0.9797 after); do not drop the restore because the weights are already restored.
plan-2026-09-19T040641-db6932ec/D-010 | 2026-09-19 | The old power_mlp/visualization.py replaces itself with the shared classification_viz module object (sys.modules swap), so the old import path keeps every public and private name and stays monkeypatchable; a from-import re-export would copy names and leave patches unread.
plan-2026-09-19T040641-db6932ec/D-012 | 2026-09-19 | Copy the weights at the end of every epoch and restore them after the figures so final_model.keras is the real last-epoch model, not the best one Keras restores at train end; best_model.keras is reloaded for the best metrics.
plan-2026-09-19T040641-db6932ec/D-013 | 2026-09-19 | build_lr_schedule always passes steps_per_epoch and warmup_steps (warmup_epochs * steps_per_epoch), because leaving steps_per_epoch unset collapsed the cosine to its 1e-5 floor within `epochs` steps; the config refuses warmup on a non-cosine schedule.
plan-2026-09-19T040641-db6932ec/D-014 | 2026-09-19 | One plain model.evaluate on the validation split before fit feeds both the summary's initial-loss ratio and the dashboard baseline (baseline_fn returns that dict), so the two consumers can never disagree.
plan-2026-09-19T040641-db6932ec/D-019 | 2026-09-19 | A local _EpochLogLine callback, placed after every callback that edits logs, writes one true per-epoch line to run.log and the console, because Keras's progress bar averages its running-mean logs a second time and prints train metrics that disagree with the CSV.
plan-2026-09-19T040641-db6932ec/D-020 | 2026-09-19 | LearningRateLogger takes an opt-in at_epoch_start flag that reads the rate at epoch begin, so the logged lr is the rate the epoch used rather than the next epoch's first-step rate; the default path stays byte-identical for PowerMLP and CapsNet.
plan-2026-09-19T040641-db6932ec/D-021 | 2026-09-19 | The train pipeline uses drop_remainder=True and steps_per_epoch_for(n, batch) = n // batch as the single step count, removing a second XLA recompile (about 20 s) on the short last batch and making the cosine end on the real last step.
plan-2026-09-19T040641-db6932ec/D-027 | 2026-09-19 | The train-pool-below-one-batch refusal lives in TrainingConfig.__post_init__ (via split_sizes and steps_per_epoch_for), so a bad --max-samples aborts before prepare_run_dir and no run directory or experiment name is burned.
plan-2026-09-19T040641-db6932ec/D-033 | 2026-09-19 | The ConvNeXt trainer defaults strides to 2, not the model classes' 4: measured on CIFAR-10, strides 2 beat strides 4 by 10.6 points (0.7206 vs 0.6148) for about 12 percent more epoch time; the model files and the MAE trainer keep 4.
plan-2026-09-19T040641-db6932ec/D-040 | 2026-09-19 | build_loss returns the stock SparseCategoricalCrossentropy at label_smoothing 0.0 and a registered serializable SmoothedSparseCategoricalCrossentropy above 0, so default runs keep the stock loss object and the a = 0 guard means something; Keras's sparse loss has no smoothing argument.
plan-2026-09-19T131351-b8d39688/D-002 | 2026-09-20 | Bfunet refusals run in three layers: pure value rules in __post_init__, a preflight in train() before anything is written (image dirs, then refuse_existing_run), and model-dependent refusals after the run directory exists, so a bad config burns no experiment name and a reused name is never merged.
plan-2026-09-19T131351-b8d39688/D-003 | 2026-09-20 | The default WarmupSchedule(CosineDecay) with the whole-horizon decay_steps is kept and pinned by a characterization test (E=100, W=10, spe=400 ends at 3.42e-5, E=8 at 4.84e-5, not the 1e-5 floor) because the published bfunet runs used it; only cosine_decay is accepted.
plan-2026-09-19T131351-b8d39688/D-004 | 2026-09-20 | warmup_epochs greater than epochs is refused but warmup_epochs equal to epochs is accepted with a WARNING that the cosine never starts, so --epochs 1 with the derived default warmup keeps working.
plan-2026-09-19T131351-b8d39688/D-005 | 2026-09-20 | restore_best_weights is set to False on the EarlyStopping instance after the shared callbacks are built, so final_model.keras is the real last epoch and best_model.keras (ModelCheckpoint on val_loss) stays the best, with no per-epoch weight copy.
plan-2026-09-19T131351-b8d39688/D-006 | 2026-09-20 | Bfunet uses the shared LearningRateLogger(at_epoch_start=True) and deletes the local LRLoggerCallback, so the CSV lr is the rate the epoch started at; the dashboard reads logs lr, the epoch-0 point is nan and the LR panel is clipped 3 decades below the peak.
plan-2026-09-19T131351-b8d39688/D-007 | 2026-09-20 | The per-epoch line is built from the true epoch logs by the shared EpochLogLine(keys) promoted from the ConvNeXt trainer, and train() attaches run.log with attach_run_log; the Keras progress bar (which double-averages) stays but the CSV and the epoch line are the reference.
plan-2026-09-19T131351-b8d39688/D-009 | 2026-09-20 | At the end of train() the best model (reloaded from best_model.keras) and the final model are scored on Kodak24 and CBSD68 at sigma 15/25/50 through eval_psnr_vs_noise.evaluate_dataset via a function-level import, recorded in results_summary.json test_eval, and a missing directory or an error never fails a finished run.
plan-2026-09-19T131351-b8d39688/D-011 | 2026-09-20 | A shape-mismatched --init-from layer is refused (naming the layers) instead of being left at random init, and the load report is recorded in the summary; a missing_in_source layer stays a WARNING plus a count.
plan-2026-09-19T131351-b8d39688/D-013 | 2026-09-20 | Only the ConvUNeXt trainer CLI is reworked: --smoke preset lives in one table keyed by argparse dest and is applied only to dests the user did not type (sentinel detection, never comparing to the parser default), so --smoke --epochs 3 runs 3 epochs; unet and bfcnn keep their own smoke handling.
plan-2026-09-19T131351-b8d39688/D-015 | 2026-09-20 | The finished results_summary.json is written LAST, after the final save and the load round-trip check, from the same head as the diverged summary, so a status ok summary exists only for a run that saved final_model.keras.
plan-2026-09-19T224205-49c8bf80/D-004 | 2026-09-21 | The segmentation trainer trains create_convunext (use_bias True, 3-channel linear head) with SparseCategoricalCrossentropy(from_logits=True), stock MeanIoU(num_classes=3, sparse_y_true=True, name="miou") and accuracy named "accuracy" so the shared dashboard draws it; deep supervision off, monitor val_loss.
plan-2026-09-19T224205-49c8bf80/D-008 | 2026-09-21 | The segmentation trainer sets restore_best_weights False on the EarlyStopping instance after create_callbacks, reloads best_model.keras for the best-weights evaluation and figures, and saves the in-memory model as final_model.keras, so final is the real last epoch.
plan-2026-09-19T224205-49c8bf80/D-009 | 2026-09-21 | One small function in src/train/common/run_summary.py reads a data-free analysis back from disk (file exists, parses, which analyzers wrote results, status ok/partial/missing/skipped plus error string) and both the segmentation and denoiser trainers use it instead of trusting the analyzer's return.
plan-2026-09-19T224205-49c8bf80/D-012 | 2026-09-21 | The minimum image size of the segmentation trainer is the analytic rule 2**depth read from CONVUNEXT_CONFIGS, refused at config time, and a test re-measures it with a real forward pass at min-1, min, min+1 for depths 3-5; the model build uses no build probe.
plan-2026-09-19T224205-49c8bf80/D-015 | 2026-09-21 | Denoiser parity with the ConvNeXt layout: model_analysis declared once in the shared BFUnetTrainingConfig (default False, True in the ConvUNeXt config), an end-of-run analyzer plus a visualizations block listing the files really written, seeded grid noise for additive noise, pass-1 PSNR clipped like passes 2-3, test_eval scoring best once when best == last, and jacobian_symmetry._NORM_EPS a plain float so importing it opens no GPU context.
plan-2026-09-19T224205-49c8bf80/D-016 | 2026-09-21 | When best_epoch equals epochs_run and the best checkpoint loaded, the test split is scored once and test_metrics_final is that same result, recorded as final_reused_best true; both metric keys stay populated, and an unloadable checkpoint falls back to scoring the in-memory model.
plan-2026-09-19T224205-49c8bf80/D-017 | 2026-09-21 | The segmentation horizontal flip is a stateless draw tf.random.stateless_uniform([], seed=[seed, position]) keyed by the element's index in the epoch's shuffled order, so same-seed runs are reproducible; rejected dropping num_parallel_calls (hides the race at a throughput cost) and Dataset.random(seed) (its stream depends on the global seed, measured).
plan-2026-09-19T224205-49c8bf80/D-026 | 2026-09-21 | The segmentation test evaluation is ONE pass (predict_on_batch per batch, loss from the compiled model.loss weighted by batch size, accuracy and mIoU read off the confusion matrix) instead of Keras evaluate plus a predict loop, cutting about 17 s per scored model; guarded by equality with keras evaluate to 1e-5.
plan-2026-09-19T224205-49c8bf80/D-027 | 2026-09-21 | run_summary.run_data_free_analysis deletes the library's empty summary_dashboard.png from the run's own model_analysis/ when the read-back status is ok and records "removed" in the analyzer block; it is safe because that function only ever builds the data-free (weights plus spectral) configuration.
plan-2026-09-19T224205-49c8bf80/D-029 | 2026-09-21 | The segmentation figure writer skips best_vs_final_predictions.png when best_epoch equals epochs_run (identical columns), records visualizations.skipped with the reason and logs it, and the same-weights branch and its label are deleted.
plan-2026-09-19T224205-49c8bf80/D-046 | 2026-09-21 | The single run_model_analysis call inside run_data_free_analysis runs under threadpool_limits(limits=1): the WeightWatcher spectral phase ran 12 BLAS threads and swung between 2.5 and 19.7 s, with one thread analyzer.seconds fell from 15-22 s to about 6 s and no weight or spectral number changed beyond the fit's own randomness.
plan-2026-09-19T224205-49c8bf80/D-047 | 2026-09-21 | An opt-in --deterministic-data flag (ConvUNeXt denoiser only, default off, field deterministic_data on the shared config) seeds both shuffles, makes the decode map deterministic and runs the crop, augment and noise maps serially, so same-command denoiser runs differ only by GPU kernel non-determinism (val PSNR range 0.028 to 0.032 dB against 0.443 dB for the default pipeline).
plan-2026-09-19T224205-49c8bf80/D-051 | 2026-09-21 | The results_summary.json range key is renamed image_range and states only what is true under every supported flag (the range of the clean images, [0, 1]); config.json keeps clip_noise and its data_range provenance string, so no key has one name and two types across the two files.
plan-2026-09-19T224205-49c8bf80/D-056 | 2026-09-21 | An opt-in --monitor {val_loss,val_miou} on the segmentation trainer (default val_loss unchanged) reaches ModelCheckpoint, EarlyStopping, best_epoch, the summary, the dashboard best marker and the mIoU curve label; built because the final weights beat the val_loss checkpoint in all three seeds (gaps 0.0097, 0.0200, 0.0049 test mIoU) under a pre-registered rule.
plan-2026-09-19T224205-49c8bf80/D-057 | 2026-09-21 | eval_psnr_vs_noise._predict_denoised pads the input to a multiple of the batch size and crops the prediction back, so each model traces its predict function once instead of twice and the tf.function retracing warning of a best != final run is gone (evaluation 12.5-13.0 s to 8.5-10.4 s; PSNR cells move up to 6.5e-5 dB with TF32).
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-005 | 2026-09-30 | The closed-form fit is the method HKAN.fit_closed_form, never an override of fit or train_step: float64 numpy, layer by layer, chunked over outputs, minimum-norm least squares at l2 == 0 and normal equations at l2 > 0, with results cast into the Keras weights outside backprop.
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-007 | 2026-09-30 | The same weights train through stock compile() and fit(): coef starts from a random initializer, mix at 1/n_in, each add_weight site gets its own initializer instance, and centers stay non-trainable in every mode.
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-018 | 2026-09-30 | The identity basis is g(d) = d on the unscaled difference, so HKANLayer uses an effective slope of 1 for it and says so in its docstring.
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-020 | 2026-09-30 | fit_closed_form assigns a data draw into the centers weight first and then reads the centers back as float64 for the solve, in every centers mode.
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-022 | 2026-09-30 | solve_linear_float64 calls np.linalg.lstsq(matrix, centered_target, rcond=None) at l2 == 0; do not pass a bare machine epsilon as the rank cutoff and do not solve the normal equations (supersedes D-019).
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-031 | 2026-09-30 | load_data returns the inputs unrounded in float64 and Keras casts on the way into the model; rounding inputs to float32 before a float64 fit made the targets inconsistent and cost a factor of about 1000 in test RMSE.
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-034 | 2026-09-30 | fit_closed_form ends with a forward pass of the built model at its own dtype and returns forward_rmse and forward_deviation_rms, logging a warning when the deviation exceeds 1e-3 of std(y) (1e-6 absolute for a constant target), because a float64 fit stored in float32 weights can compute a different model.
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-035 | 2026-09-30 | SEED_DOMAIN_TAG is the LAST entry of the layer's seed list, because numpy's default_rng([seed, 0, 0]) equals default_rng(seed) (trailing zeros are dropped) and centers would otherwise equal the caller's own stream.
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-036 | 2026-09-30 | solve_closed_form returns block_r2 = 0.0 for every block when the target is constant, instead of a ratio against a zero total variance (which gave -2e30).
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-037 | 2026-09-30 | HKANLayer.build raises ValueError when the compute dtype is not float32 or float64, naming the dtype, the policy and the two supported policies, because the float64 solve is only meaningful against those weights.
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-039 | 2026-09-30 | Constructor defaults are slope=5.0, l2_block=0.01, l2_mix=0.01, the best passing candidate in a 180-fit sweep (worst forward deviation 5.5e-04 of std, threshold 1e-3), because the reference defaults made the float32 model compute something other than its own fit once a hidden layer existed.
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-041 | 2026-09-30 | Every synthetic target and its inputs are min-max scaled to [0, 1] with TRAIN statistics, TF2 noise is added after scaling, and TF4 keeps the standard Salomon +0.1 r (the paper prints -0.1 r) as a declared reading.
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-042 | 2026-09-30 | best_checkpoint has one shape in every backprop mode and a diverged run finishes with source none; two-phase runs save closed_form_model.keras before compile() and write best_model.keras only when an epoch beats the closed-form start.
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-043 | 2026-09-30 | fit_rows is recorded from the rows actually fitted on, the validation split seed list ends in its own non-zero tag, the trainer defaults are imported from the package constants, and bad arguments are refused at parse time before anything is written.
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-049 | 2026-09-30 | HKAN.__init__ passes its dtype policy to every HKANLayer, so HKAN(dtype="float64") gives float64 layers (deviation 3.1e-16) and mixed_float16 or bfloat16 set on the constructor are refused at build like the global policy.
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-050 | 2026-09-30 | The forward check's batch is derived from the solve's byte budget through HKANLayer.solve_chunk_outputs, so chunk_size bounds memory again (peak 3322 MB to 1110 MB on the review probe) and the largest weight is taken with default=0.0 for a frozen model.
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-051 | 2026-09-30 | is_constant_target treats a target as constant when ptp(y) <= 4 * eps64 * max|y|, a relative test that holds at every magnitude (one ulp is constant, 64 ulps never is).
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-052 | 2026-09-30 | With --val-fraction 0 the fit rows are passed as validation_data, so the val_loss monitor is an exact end-of-epoch MSE on the same rows as the closed-form start threshold, and best_checkpoint records monitor_rows.
plan-2026-09-30T082355-4d999dbc/D-053 | 2026-09-30 | The parser refuses any --slope that is not finite and positive, before anything is written.
plan-2026-10-02T084508-dd2c07ac/D-008 | 2026-10-02 | LightGlue.call is the static masked path (all layers, per-layer log-assignments and confidences) and match() is a separate eager batch-1 method with early stop and pruning; an equivalence test with the knobs disabled ties them together.
plan-2026-10-02T084508-dd2c07ac/D-009 | 2026-10-02 | Keep the Dense(3d) qkv output layout (heads, head_dim, 3) and sublayer names 1:1 with the torch state dict so weight conversion is a transpose only; no converter is shipped.
plan-2026-10-02T084508-dd2c07ac/D-012 | 2026-10-02 | LightGlueLoss.call returns the per-sample NLL from packed int labels plus log_assignments; the full objective (NLL plus confidence_weight times confidence BCE, with real-keypoint masks) is compute(...) for add_loss.
plan-2026-10-02T084508-dd2c07ac/D-013 | 2026-10-02 | KeypointMatchMetric takes packed labels and log_assignments, reuses unpack_matches, and is updated inside the pipeline call so it reaches the stock fit logs.
plan-2026-10-02T084508-dd2c07ac/D-014 | 2026-10-02 | Stock fit with no custom train_step: the pipeline wrapper computes labels and the full loss in call, registers it with add_loss, updates metrics there, and the best checkpoint saves the LightGlue alone.
plan-2026-10-02T084508-dd2c07ac/D-016 | 2026-10-02 | Dustbin labels follow glue-factory's one-direction rule (forward-only min for image 1 side, backward-only min for image 0 side, above neg_threshold), superseding D-011's max rule.
plan-2026-10-02T084508-dd2c07ac/D-017 | 2026-10-02 | Pair views are border-free warped patches of a 1.5x source frame, shrunk about the centre until all four corners sit inside the frame, so no zero-filled borders appear in training views.
plan-2026-10-02T084508-dd2c07ac/D-018 | 2026-10-02 | Token-confidence BCE is computed from logits (max(z,0) - z*t + log1p(exp(-|z|))), exposed via return_logits and token_logits0/1, so the gradient does not die at saturation.
plan-2026-10-02T084508-dd2c07ac/D-019 | 2026-10-02 | build_optimizer excludes the positional-encoding frequency kernel from AdamW weight decay by variable (var_list plus the name patterns, since the call replaces the earlier one).
plan-2026-10-02T084508-dd2c07ac/D-020 | 2026-10-02 | The singular-homography test runs on the size-normalised matrix diag(1/w1,1/h1,1) H diag(w0,h0,1), and the inverse is taken from it, so the test is scale-independent across image sizes.

ssp-2026-10-05/D-001 | 2026-10-05 | pass@k's binomial ratio goes through scipy.special.gammaln, never math.comb: C(n,k) overflows float64 above n~1300 and gammaln is vectorised, so pass_at_k_curve is one call instead of an n_k-length loop. The ratio is clamped to [0,1] because it is a probability and cannot leave that interval.
ssp-2026-10-05/D-002 | 2026-10-05 | 0*log(0) is evaluated as 0 by a `where` guard, NOT by clipping p into [eps, 1-eps]: clipping makes binary_entropy(0) return positive noise, so fully-unsolved groups collect a little entropy and max_entropy_weight keeps a non-zero weight on rows that carry no signal.
ssp-2026-10-05/D-003 | 2026-10-05 | Expert fusion accumulates in float64 and casts back once. This bounds but does NOT remove order dependence (float addition is not associative in any precision): MEASURED reordering perturbation on 5 specialists x 3 vars of (500,40) float32 is at most 1e-6 relative, where the float32 sum is order-dependent by construction. The bound, not bit-equality, is the guarantee.
ssp-2026-10-05/D-004 | 2026-10-05 | fuse_specialists short-circuits to a copy when every specialist is byte-identical, so uniform fusion of identical inputs is the bit-exact identity; averaging N copies in float64 rounds to within an ulp, and every atol=0 comparison against the original would inherit that. Gated behind a head-and-tail fingerprint so the non-identical case costs no full pass.
ssp-2026-10-05/D-005 | 2026-10-05 | select_specialists breaks score ties toward the LOWEST checkpoint index (np.argmax already does, so the rule is free). Stated because a tie means two checkpoints measured identically, and a selection depending on dict ordering is not re-derivable from a saved score matrix.
ssp-2026-10-05/D-006 | 2026-10-05 | max_entropy_deviation uses log of (1-p), NOT log1p of (1-p): log1p(1-p) is log(2-p), which is neither Bernoulli KL term. It raises nothing and at p=0.5 returned 0.549 instead of 0, so the weight peaked at the wrong p and the 'maximum weight at maximum uncertainty' premise silently did not hold.
ssp-2026-10-05/D-007 | 2026-10-05 | A per-group weight vector and a per-response advantage are each re-inserted with an explicit axis before broadcasting: group_success_rate drops the group axis, so (batch,)*(batch,G) compares batch against G and raises unless equal, and a (batch,) per-response advantage cannot broadcast to (batch,T) at all. Same defect, two directions.
ssp-2026-10-05/D-008 | 2026-10-05 | spectrum_sampling_weights reports the max/min weight ratio only over POSITIVE weights: `proportional` on a vector containing a zero yields an exact zero and the ratio against it is ~1e308, a log line full of digits that says nothing. Zero weights are reported as a count instead.
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-007 | 2026-10-12 | Language-model pretrained=<local .keras path> is backbone-only: it skips the task_head/ layers and builds the model at a fixed small length instead of the old random int32 dummy forward, so swapping heads is a by-name backbone load.
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-008 | 2026-10-12 | DistilBERT.load_pretrained_weights branches on include_top: a headed model takes the by-name route that skips task_head, a headless model keeps the whole-file load.
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-010 | 2026-10-12 | FNet has no sequence_length knob; load_pretrained_weights builds an unbuilt model at the checkpoint's own length read from the .keras archive build_config (falling back to min(128, max_position_embeddings)).
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-011 | 2026-10-12 | TreeTransformer under include_top=True creates no lm_head and returns only the finalized head output; the backbone is every weight except lm_head_projection/ and task_head/, and the head is fed the mask the blocks use.
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-012 | 2026-10-12 | Decoder heads default to last-token pooling and pool under head_attention_mask (caller mask, else input_ids != pad_token_id) while the decoder sees only the caller mask; pad_token_id is a new round-tripped arg; the pretrained build is a small fixed length with no RNG; no logits or untied lm_head are made under a head.
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-013 | 2026-10-12 | Mamba and Mamba2 share one loader (mamba_v1.load_local_checkpoint) that builds first with no RNG or forward; Mamba2 gains pretrained and build() so both from_variant routes behave the same.
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-014 | 2026-10-12 | The knob-sensitivity oracle is fixed, not the test: an explicit extract gets the raw output and the logits unwrap applies only when no extract is given.
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-016 | 2026-10-12 | The precision-arm family keeps headless subject builders and adds HEADED_SUBJECTS read by the round-trip arm, with a mixed-float16 head test and explicit-build pins re-measured on the headed build; no dtype_exempt_outputs entry changed.
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-020 | 2026-10-12 | One load rule for the encoders: every backbone layer loads or the load raises, only the embedding may stay fresh and only when the checkpoint's own vocab_size differs, and task_head is never copied; a raise copies nothing.
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-021 | 2026-10-12 | BERT refuses an over-long sequence before any variable exists (static) or through a tf.debugging assertion under control dependencies (dynamic axis), and drops cached train/predict functions on refusal so the next call traces fresh. Code now lives in src/dl_techniques/utils/sequence_length_guard.py (moved out of bert/model.py by plan-2026-10-11T170958-dc4d2202 step 3; shared by every language class with a position limit).
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-022 | 2026-10-12 | TreeTransformer derives its vocabulary skip from the checkpoint and applies the strict load rule with lm_head allowed unloaded, since lm_head is a top (D-011) a headed checkpoint does not hold.
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-024 | 2026-10-12 | GPT2 pretrained, headed or headless, takes one route (build, then by-name strict load skipping task_head with lm_head allowed unloaded); the shared load-rule test file is table-driven and ignores one Keras warning so a misfit arm sees production behavior.
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-025 | 2026-10-12 | Mamba and Mamba2 use the vocabulary-aware by-name load for headed models or a vocabulary change, and keep the strict whole-file load only for a headless same-vocabulary checkpoint (later removed by D-031).
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-026 | 2026-10-12 | TopoLM and WaveFieldLLM take one by-name strict route after the build, with the token embedding and lm_head allowed unloaded on a vocabulary change; the shared test randomizer leaves integer weights alone.
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-027 | 2026-10-12 | load_weights_from_checkpoint compares the layers of the one model a functional wrapper holds (InputLayer plus a single keras.Model is unwrapped), so a backbone moves between classifier wrappers and headed models.
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-030 | 2026-10-12 | BERT's length refusal also holds under XLA: a zero-sum term sized by max_position_embeddings minus the length is added inside the assertion's control dependency, so an over-long length fails at compile time naming that node (D-032 corrects the claim: the iteration counter is unchanged only under XLA). Code now lives in src/dl_techniques/utils/sequence_length_guard.py (moved out of bert/model.py by plan-2026-10-11T170958-dc4d2202 step 3; shared by every language class with a position limit).
plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-031 | 2026-10-12 | The shared load rule lives in utils/weight_transfer.py and is complete-or-raise in both directions: a weight-bearing checkpoint layer with no target layer raises, and the vocabulary exemption covers only vocab-shaped variables via vocab_change(); all ten classes call load_backbone_from_checkpoint.
plan-2026-10-11T170958-dc4d2202/D-005 | 2026-10-13 | Zamba2 and HNet gain include_top/task_config on the GPT2/TopoLM shape: headless output stays the tied-logits tensor, the final-norm output also feeds the task head, HNet keeps its ratio loss in both tops and builds no untied lm_head under a head, and pretrained=<path> goes through the shared load rule by name and strict.
plan-2026-10-11T170958-dc4d2202/D-006 | 2026-10-13 | One module utils/sequence_length_guard.py gives every class with a real position limit a build-time static refusal that also resets the cached step functions plus a dynamic zero-sum gate; BLT warns and builds on load instead of refusing so old archives recording an over-long shape stay loadable.
plan-2026-10-11T170958-dc4d2202/D-007 | 2026-10-13 | Vocabulary tables are identified by the variable path suffix each class declares (VocabChange.tables, required) AND the shape test, so a position table resized from exactly the old vocabulary size raises instead of staying random with a warning.
plan-2026-10-11T170958-dc4d2202/D-009 | 2026-10-13 | build_model_task_head and task_config_to_dict take the model's vocab_size: an unset generation-head vocabulary is filled from the model and a different explicit value raises ValueError, with no head class modified.
plan-2026-10-11T170958-dc4d2202/D-010 | 2026-10-13 | The four finetune trainers rebuild the headed class from the checkpoint config through one shared builder and a per-layer freeze helper in train/common/nlp.py, a failed load raises (no random fallback), and the saved head layer is named task_head.
plan-2026-10-11T170958-dc4d2202/D-014 | 2026-10-13 | The TreeTransformer group-attention sqrt runs in float32 (finite fp16 gradients) and the auto-named sub-layers of Tree, Mamba, Mamba2 and the MoE experts/gating get explicit names; this is load-neutral because archives are keyed by the attribute tree, and the matching waiver entries were deleted.
plan-2026-10-11T170958-dc4d2202/D-015 | 2026-10-13 | The shared load rule reads through a one-model functional wrapper on both sides (target unwrapped, checkpoint vocab_size/include_top from the wrapped model's config), so create_qwen3, create_qwen3_next and create_gemma3 accept pretrained=<path> for either a wrapper or a bare-class checkpoint.

## Retired anchors

plan-2026-08-14T233721-d4f9beb2/D-029 | RETIRED 2026-10-13 | Its three `# DECISION` comments lived at the `pooling_strategy` check of `create_qwen3_classification` (`src/dl_techniques/models/language/qwen/qwen3.py`), `create_qwen3_next_classification` (`qwen3_next.py`) and `create_gemma3_classification` (`src/dl_techniques/models/language/gemma/gemma3.py`), and were removed with those factories by step 4 of `plan-2026-10-13T035756-5dd4d94d` (`create_<model>` now returns the class; a head comes only from `include_top=True` with a `task_config`). The RULE survives on the class: a headed Qwen3, Qwen3Next or Gemma3 pools `"last"` by default (`default_pooling="last"` in each `__init__`, anchored `plan-2026-10-11T004932-b83935e1/D-012`), pinned by `tests/test_models/test_qwen/test_causal_classification_pooling.py` and `tests/test_models/test_gemma/test_causal_classification_pooling.py` (`task_head.pooling_type == "last"` plus the value probes).
plan-2026-08-14T233721-d4f9beb2/D-032 | RETIRED 2026-10-13 | Its `# DECISION` comment lived at the `model.compile(...)` call inside `create_hierarchical_reasoning_model` (`src/dl_techniques/models/language/hrm/model.py`) and was removed with that compile by step 13 of `plan-2026-10-13T035756-5dd4d94d` (D-015: the factory only delegates to `HierarchicalReasoningModel.from_variant`, which returns a built, uncompiled model; `optimizer`/`learning_rate`/`loss` are refused). The RULE it carried survives at the caller: a `fit()` compile supervises `{"logits": StableMaxCrossEntropy()}` only, because the Q term couples two model outputs and cannot be a per-output Keras loss, and ACT supervision needs `src/train/hrm/train_hrm.py`. Pinned by `tests/test_models/test_hrm/test_act_contract.py::TestTheFactoryLeavesTheCompileToTheCaller` and `tests/test_models/test_hrm/test_the_q_head_trains_where_it_is_supposed_to.py`. The D-051 anchor (build, not a dummy forward) moved with the build into `from_variant`, verbatim.
plan-2026-10-13T035756-5dd4d94d/D-004 | 2026-10-13 | An archive whose recorded build shape is over the position limit loads every weight with a logged warning: RecordedBuildShapeMixin sets a flag during build_from_config and the guard logs and returns while it is set, instead of raising into Keras's swallowed build.
plan-2026-10-13T035756-5dd4d94d/D-005 | 2026-10-13 | CausalLanguageModel and MaskedLanguageModel builds drop their own cached step functions and re-raise a SequenceLengthError (a ValueError) so the wrapper stays unbuilt and usable after a refused first call, while every other backbone error keeps the warn-and-continue path.
plan-2026-10-13T035756-5dd4d94d/D-006 | 2026-10-13 | The decoder head mask stays all ones when there is neither an attention mask nor a pad_token_id; do not restore a 0 default because id 0 is a real token in the GPT2 and Qwen vocabularies (origin plan-2026-10-11T170958-dc4d2202/D-008).
plan-2026-10-13T035756-5dd4d94d/D-007 | 2026-10-13 | HNet's vocabulary is read from the archive's arch_config.vocab_size on the shared load path, never from the top-level config or a class default (origin plan-2026-10-11T170958-dc4d2202/D-017).
plan-2026-10-13T035756-5dd4d94d/D-010 | 2026-10-13 | Integer weights are int64 (utils/dtype_policy.INDEX_WEIGHT_DTYPE) because TF pins an int32 variable to the host and a jit-compiled fit on GPU cannot read it; PTRM rollouts run all D steps from the init states so injected noise reaches every item.
plan-2026-10-13T035756-5dd4d94d/D-014 | 2026-10-13 | Qwen3 and Qwen3Next experts take moe_intermediate_size verbatim as their hidden width and a dense Qwen3Next (num_experts=1) builds a dense FFN of that width at each MoE slot, so old shapes refuse to load instead of loading partially.
plan-2026-10-13T035756-5dd4d94d/D-015 | 2026-10-13 | Language create_* factories with a variant only delegate to from_variant (HRM no longer compiles, PTRM has one create_ptrm), and from_variant takes pretrained, so the factory-delegation waivers are gone.
plan-2026-10-13T035756-5dd4d94d/D-017 | 2026-10-13 | TransformerLayer warns twice and separately when moe_config is given: once when an expert's hidden width falls back to intermediate_size, once when a caller's ffn_type or ffn_args are ignored; Qwen3 passes ffn_type and ffn_args to dense layers only so its MoE construction stays silent.
plan-2026-10-13T035756-5dd4d94d/D-018 | 2026-10-13 | PTRM inference follows arXiv:2605.19943: rollouts are selected by the halting logit q_halt (use_q_continue is opt-in), noise goes into z_L only, and PTRMTrainer.train_step is frozen beside its HRM and TRM siblings.
plan-2026-10-13T035756-5dd4d94d/D-019 | 2026-10-13 | PTRM mlp_* variants are removed because they built attention models, preset labels are measured trainable weights, and the trainer honours the preset variant (D-015 anchors at the HRM and PTRM factories).
plan-2026-10-13T035756-5dd4d94d/D-020 | 2026-10-13 | SequencePooling gathers a positional mode's selected vector with a flat-row keras.ops.take instead of take_along_axis, whose TF broadcast_dynamic_shape makes XLA on GPU refuse a predict at a second sequence length.
plan-2026-10-13T035756-5dd4d94d/D-021 | 2026-10-13 | SequencePooling top_k_mean and top_k_max serve a second sequence length under XLA on GPU: an unknown length pads top_k positions below the mask sentinel so k is constant and padded picks are marked invalid, while a static length keeps k = minimum(top_k, seq_len).
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-003 | 2026-10-14 | neural_hmm inference runs on one uniform (B,T,K,K) log_A where log_A[:, t] is the transition into step t, so the forward alpha sits on the source axis and one path serves static and input-dependent transitions.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-004 | 2026-10-14 | The neural_hmm objective lives in the model's compute_loss override (per-valid-step NLL, or logZ minus gold path score), not in a Loss object, because the loss needs the mask, the gold labels and log_A together; compile(loss=...) is ignored.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-005 | 2026-10-14 | The neural_hmm encoder is built only when something reads it (autoregressive context, input-dependent transitions or discriminative mode); otherwise self.encoder is None, so no preset carries a component that receives no gradient.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-006 | 2026-10-14 | The neural_hmm trainer's data splits draw from [seed, tag] random streams; do not reuse one stream across splits, or train and test sequences correlate.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-009 | 2026-10-14 | Padding is a contiguous valid prefix: a padded step freezes alpha and contributes neither emission nor transition, so padded logZ equals truncated logZ exactly and the gold path score matches; interior gaps are documented unsupported, not runtime-checked.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-010 | 2026-10-14 | Emission density math, transition log_softmax and all of inference.py run in float32 under mixed_float16, because the Gaussian log-density overflows float16 and logsumexp over -inf rows gives NaN; the mask is built in the compute dtype.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-014 | 2026-10-14 | Every neural_hmm recursion runs through keras.ops.while_loop with maximum_iterations=T, because a Python loop over T breaks predict at a second sequence length and ops.scan fails on Keras 3.8 (multi-xs) and under XLA gradients; do not replace it with either.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-016 | 2026-10-14 | Recurrent encoder layers get no dropout= argument (Keras 3.8 cuDNN LSTM/GRU apply it at inference) and a row with no valid step gets step 0 marked valid (cuDNN asserts on an all-padding mask in graph mode); both exist only because of framework defects a CPU run cannot show.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-017 | 2026-10-14 | Padded encoder steps are replaced with keras.ops.where, never multiplied by the mask (NaN propagates through multiplication), and the conv path re-zeroes before every convolution so valid outputs do not depend on the padding length.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-018 | 2026-10-14 | neural_hmm is registered in the shared precision subjects with _sub(...) and expected_compute_dtype="float32"; do not exempt all three outputs (the arm then fails itself as vacuous) or pass None (switches part 2 off).
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-019 | 2026-10-14 | The io-hmm encoder and the segmenter's pi_logits are exactly unmoved after one fit step by design (zero-initialized context projection; forced start state); they are named two-sided waivers in ONE_STEP_UNMOVED, each backed by a follow-up test, and must not be widened.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-020 | 2026-10-14 | The emission head calls create_ffn_layer with the literal type "mlp", because a variable-type call site would raise the repo-wide dynamic factory call-site ceiling (15) that an older plan's ruling forbids raising.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-021 | 2026-10-14 | The neural_hmm trainer uses EarlyStopping(restore_best_weights=True), reloads best_model.keras, records the reload difference and scores the reloaded model, instead of keeping a second copy of the best weights in a hand-written callback.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-022 | 2026-10-14 | The autoregressive context right-shifts the observations by one step so step t never sees its own symbol; the guard is a normalization test (sum of p(x) over all sequences is 1) plus a one-step liveness arm, and a state outside [0, K) at a valid step is scored loudly rather than as 0.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-025 | 2026-10-14 | The NeuralHMM constructor refuses a non-default value for an argument the chosen configuration never reads, naming the argument and why; default values stay accepted so from_config round trips.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-026 | 2026-10-14 | NeuralHMM.from_config resets a stale non-default unread argument to its default with a logged warning instead of refusing, so archives written before the refusal still load; construction from user code stays strict.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-027 | 2026-10-14 | An out-of-range symbol at a valid step gets a neutral log_emit (like a padded step) and the penalty is added once per offending step outside the forward pass, because a -1e9 sentinel inside the potentials makes float32 posteriors sum to K.
plan-2026-10-13T192257-1bb3bf1f/D-029 | 2026-10-14 | NeuralHMM refuses n_states < 2 for every configuration, because with one state the initial and transition distributions are constants and no configuration can train them.

Each stub line names an anchor id that resolved to NOTHING at 139c7088e: either its plan has no `plans/<plan-id>/` directory, no `plans/DECISIONS.md` section and no line in this manifest (182 ids of 88 plans, 233 source sites, `validate-plan.mjs` `[anchor-unknown-plan]`), or its plan resolves here but the cited D-number has no line (35 ids of 19 plans, 52 sites, `[anchor-orphan]`). Stubs are NOT decision records; do not read one as evidence a choice was made. A stub also has a cost: it makes the cited number resolve whether or not it was the right number, so where a source anchor mistyped a D-number, the validator's orphan error for that typo is now silenced (`plan-2026-10-14T062616-81938e81` findings F-10 H1b). The plan directories are gone, so no typo can be checked any more. `bootstrap.mjs retire` was not used on any of these ids: it stamps `[STALE]` on rules that still bind.*
*Re-derive the set (repo root): run `node <skill-path>/scripts/validate-plan.mjs` at 139c7088e with this manifest as it stood before the block, and collect the `plan-id/D-NNN` of every `[anchor-unknown-plan]` and `[anchor-orphan]` line, ERROR and WARN. Script: `make_stubs.py` in that plan's scratchpad (`iter2/anchors/`).*

*Minted ids, appended 2026-10-22 by plan-2026-10-14T062616-81938e81 step 14 (D-122 item 3). Five anchor families carried a prefix that is not a plan-id, so `validate-plan.mjs` could not see them at all (`[anchor-badprefix]`). Each family got a conforming id, `plan-<date>T<HHMMSS>-<first 8 hex of sha256 of the old prefix string>`: the date is the family's own; the time is the one its old prefix or plan directory carried (`T135600` mcsplit, `T140000` from `plans/plan-2026-10-05T140000-ssp-spectrum-to-signal/`) or else the UTC time of the commit that first wrote the prefix into source (de3b4d793, 96ac41f8b, 7e30ee27d). The source prefixes were rewritten in the same step. Where the old prefix had lines in this manifest, each is copied unchanged under the minted id; the old lines stay (append-only). `<gen-metrics-2026-10-18>` and `plan-2026-10-05_warped-detector` never had a line, so their ids are stubs.*

plan-2026-08-14T135600-b092fcc4/D-001 | 2026-08-14 | SPLIT `models/mobile_clip_v2/` in two: the assembled MCi tower becomes its own standalone model package `models/fastvit/` (`FastVitImageEncoder` + `MCI_VARIANTS`, class names UNCHANGED so every `Custom>` registry key survives), and the CLIP model moves to `models/mobile_clip/mobile_clip_v2.py` beside v1. `layers/fastvit/` does NOT move — the 8 block primitives stay in `layers/` under the factory-first reuse policy. AMENDS plan-2026-08-13T183738-24486492/D-001: the `MobileClipTextEncoder` reuse is now an IN-PACKAGE import (`from .components import ...`), not a cross-package one; the two-adapter-site causal-mask invariant that forbids copying it is unchanged. [Minted id: copied unchanged from the non-conforming `plan-2026-08-14T135600-mcsplit/D-001` line (1326-line manifest, line 180); source prefix `plan-2026-08-14T135600-mcsplit` rewritten to `plan-2026-08-14T135600-b092fcc4` by plan-2026-10-14T062616-81938e81 step 14.]
plan-2026-08-14T135600-b092fcc4/D-002 | 2026-08-14 | Populate `models/mobile_clip/__init__.py` with a curated `__all__` exporting both models' classes and factories, but DELIBERATELY NOT `MODEL_VARIANTS`: v1's is the class attribute `MobileClipModel.MODEL_VARIANTS` (`b`/`s0`/`s1`/`s2`) and v2's is module-level (`mobileclip2_s*`), with disjoint keys and with `mci0`/`mci1`/`mci2` meaning real MCi rows in v2 and `keras.applications` stand-ins in v1. A package-level re-export would make that collision invisible. [Minted id: copied unchanged from the non-conforming `plan-2026-08-14T135600-mcsplit/D-002` line (1326-line manifest, line 181); source prefix `plan-2026-08-14T135600-mcsplit` rewritten to `plan-2026-08-14T135600-b092fcc4` by plan-2026-10-14T062616-81938e81 step 14.]
plan-2026-08-14T135600-b092fcc4/D-003 | 2026-08-14 | Merge the two READMEs rather than keeping a v1-only and a v2-only document: `models/mobile_clip/README.md` covers both generations with an explicit v1-vs-v2 table and a §17 "which should I use", and `models/fastvit/README.md` is new and owns the MCi tower + deviations X-1/X-2/X-3/X-5 in full (mobile_clip summarizes and links). Also REPLACED v1's `faghri2023mobileclip` BibTeX entry, whose author list, title, venue and year were all fabricated, with the real Vasu et al. CVPR 2024 citation. plan-2026-09-06T021835-fea022cf/D-001 | 2026-09-06 | `models/vision/convunext/model.py`'s Gabor stem is the paper's TRAINABLE cross-channel `Conv2D` warm start (`create_gabor_conv2d`, Ozbulak & Ekenel SIU 2018), a deliberate reversal of `plan_2026-06-19_ed071c02/D-001`'s frozen depthwise bank; `gabor_filters` becomes the stem's OUTPUT CHANNEL COUNT, the no-projection rule becomes `gabor_filters == initial_filters`, the per-channel front-end is given up, `results/bfconvunext_repro` and `results/bfconvunext_d3` are knowingly broken, and `use_bias=False` stays HARDCODED because positive homogeneity comes from the absence of bias, not from channel summing (measured over 6 inits: 2.54e-07..5.08e-07 bias-free vs 1.75e-01..1.02e+00 with a 0.3 stem bias). NOT an iterative-planner plan: standalone task, no plan directory. [Minted id: copied unchanged from the non-conforming `standalone-2026-09-06-gabor-warm-start/D-001` line (1326-line manifest, line 968); source prefix `standalone-2026-09-06-gabor-warm-start` rewritten to `plan-2026-09-06T021835-fea022cf` by plan-2026-10-14T062616-81938e81 step 14.]

plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa/D-001 | 2026-10-05 | pass@k's binomial ratio goes through scipy.special.gammaln, never math.comb: C(n,k) overflows float64 above n~1300 and gammaln is vectorised, so pass_at_k_curve is one call instead of an n_k-length loop. The ratio is clamped to [0,1] because it is a probability and cannot leave that interval. [Minted id: copied unchanged from the non-conforming `ssp-2026-10-05/D-001` line (1326-line manifest, line 1260); source prefix `<ssp-2026-10-05>` rewritten to `plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa` by plan-2026-10-14T062616-81938e81 step 14.]
plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa/D-002 | 2026-10-05 | 0*log(0) is evaluated as 0 by a `where` guard, NOT by clipping p into [eps, 1-eps]: clipping makes binary_entropy(0) return positive noise, so fully-unsolved groups collect a little entropy and max_entropy_weight keeps a non-zero weight on rows that carry no signal. [Minted id: copied unchanged from the non-conforming `ssp-2026-10-05/D-002` line (1326-line manifest, line 1261); source prefix `<ssp-2026-10-05>` rewritten to `plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa` by plan-2026-10-14T062616-81938e81 step 14.]
plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa/D-003 | 2026-10-05 | Expert fusion accumulates in float64 and casts back once. This bounds but does NOT remove order dependence (float addition is not associative in any precision): MEASURED reordering perturbation on 5 specialists x 3 vars of (500,40) float32 is at most 1e-6 relative, where the float32 sum is order-dependent by construction. The bound, not bit-equality, is the guarantee. [Minted id: copied unchanged from the non-conforming `ssp-2026-10-05/D-003` line (1326-line manifest, line 1262); source prefix `<ssp-2026-10-05>` rewritten to `plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa` by plan-2026-10-14T062616-81938e81 step 14.]
plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa/D-004 | 2026-10-05 | fuse_specialists short-circuits to a copy when every specialist is byte-identical, so uniform fusion of identical inputs is the bit-exact identity; averaging N copies in float64 rounds to within an ulp, and every atol=0 comparison against the original would inherit that. Gated behind a head-and-tail fingerprint so the non-identical case costs no full pass. [Minted id: copied unchanged from the non-conforming `ssp-2026-10-05/D-004` line (1326-line manifest, line 1263); source prefix `<ssp-2026-10-05>` rewritten to `plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa` by plan-2026-10-14T062616-81938e81 step 14.]
plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa/D-005 | 2026-10-05 | select_specialists breaks score ties toward the LOWEST checkpoint index (np.argmax already does, so the rule is free). Stated because a tie means two checkpoints measured identically, and a selection depending on dict ordering is not re-derivable from a saved score matrix. [Minted id: copied unchanged from the non-conforming `ssp-2026-10-05/D-005` line (1326-line manifest, line 1264); source prefix `<ssp-2026-10-05>` rewritten to `plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa` by plan-2026-10-14T062616-81938e81 step 14.]
plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa/D-006 | 2026-10-05 | max_entropy_deviation uses log of (1-p), NOT log1p of (1-p): log1p(1-p) is log(2-p), which is neither Bernoulli KL term. It raises nothing and at p=0.5 returned 0.549 instead of 0, so the weight peaked at the wrong p and the 'maximum weight at maximum uncertainty' premise silently did not hold. [Minted id: copied unchanged from the non-conforming `ssp-2026-10-05/D-006` line (1326-line manifest, line 1265); source prefix `<ssp-2026-10-05>` rewritten to `plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa` by plan-2026-10-14T062616-81938e81 step 14.]
plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa/D-007 | 2026-10-05 | A per-group weight vector and a per-response advantage are each re-inserted with an explicit axis before broadcasting: group_success_rate drops the group axis, so (batch,)*(batch,G) compares batch against G and raises unless equal, and a (batch,) per-response advantage cannot broadcast to (batch,T) at all. Same defect, two directions. [Minted id: copied unchanged from the non-conforming `ssp-2026-10-05/D-007` line (1326-line manifest, line 1266); source prefix `<ssp-2026-10-05>` rewritten to `plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa` by plan-2026-10-14T062616-81938e81 step 14.]
plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa/D-008 | 2026-10-05 | spectrum_sampling_weights reports the max/min weight ratio only over POSITIVE weights: `proportional` on a vector containing a zero yields an exact zero and the ratio against it is ~1e308, a log line full of digits that says nothing. Zero weights are reported as a count instead. [Minted id: copied unchanged from the non-conforming `ssp-2026-10-05/D-008` line (1326-line manifest, line 1267); source prefix `<ssp-2026-10-05>` rewritten to `plan-2026-10-05T140000-38ca11aa` by plan-2026-10-14T062616-81938e81 step 14.]

plan_2026-06-03_bc986e52/D-002 | [BACKFILL 2026-10-22] | Rationale recovered from the in-source DECISION comment (src/dl_techniques/analyzer/spectral_metrics.py:1087); NOT a decision record, a stub's reading aid: MP edge uses (1 + 1/sqrt(Q))^2 (WeightWatcher RMT_Util.calc_lambda_plus/minus), NOT (1 + sqrt(Q))^2. Q=N/M with N the LARGER dim, so 1/sqrt(Q)=sqrt(M/N)<=1. Do NOT revert to sqrt(Q) — that overestimates the edge by a factor for every rectangular layer. Authoritative: findings/ww-authoritative-reference.md A/B.
plan_2026-06-03_bc986e52/D-003 | [BACKFILL 2026-10-22] | Rationale recovered from the in-source DECISION comment (src/dl_techniques/analyzer/spectral_metrics.py:872); NOT a decision record, a stub's reading aid: Tracy-Widom per WeightWatcher remove_traps.identify_trap_mode_indices: TW = (1/sqrt(Q))*bulk_max^(2/3)*M^(-2/3); the edge is raised to TW via + sqrt(TW) (NOT + TW). c_TW retained as a tunable multiplier on sqrt(TW) (default 1.0 = WW-exact); do NOT reintroduce the old c_TW*sigma^2*M^(-2/3) linear form.
plan_2026-06-03_bc986e52/D-004 | [BACKFILL 2026-10-22] | Rationale recovered from the in-source DECISION comment (src/dl_techniques/analyzer/spectral_metrics.py:646); NOT a decision record, a stub's reading aid: do NOT use cumsum(log)+searchsorted — that array is non-monotonic so searchsorted is undefined. WW finds the largest-eigenvalue tail whose product crosses 1.0 (descending). Boundary lambda_min = sorted_asc[len - tail_count]. Δλ_min MUST stay signed (prior D-006 / SETOL §7.3) — do NOT wrap in abs(); the sign IS the ERG boundary diagnostic (<0, ≈0 at the critical point, >0 normal).
plan_2026-06-03_bc986e52/D-008 | [BACKFILL 2026-10-22] | Rationale recovered from the in-source DECISION comment (src/dl_techniques/analyzer/spectral_metrics.py:416); NOT a decision record, a stub's reading aid: small-N (<20) uses the (n-1) bias correction + KS-tail-size penalty. plan_2026-06-03_bc986e52/D-010 | [BACKFILL 2026-10-22] | Rationale recovered from the in-source DECISION comment (src/dl_techniques/analyzer/spectral_utils.py:189); NOT a decision record, a stub's reading aid: Normalization layers (BatchNorm/LayerNorm/RMSNorm/etc.) carry only 1-D scale/shift (gamma/beta) vectors; their ESD is degenerate, so spectral analysis is skipped explicitly (previously a silent fall-through). plan_2026-06-04_7ff8ea8b/D-002 | [BACKFILL 2026-10-22] | Rationale recovered from the in-source DECISION comment (src/train/vae/train_vae.py:96); NOT a decision record, a stub's reading aid: for hypersphere modes the decoder consumes the ON-SPHERE latent z = radius * normalize(z_mean + eps); the raw z_mean is the UNNORMALIZED mean and can have ||z_mean|| >> 4, so the old hard [-4,4] clamp rendered a healthy direction-spread as a "collapsed point". plan-2026-10-14T062616-81938e81/D-001 | 2026-10-23 | Make the classifier a linear `Dense(num_classes, name='classifier')`. `call` returns `{'logits': z, 'probabilities': keras.ops.softmax(z, axis=-1)}`, computed in the compute dtype exactly as the NLP head does. Under `mixed_float16`, Step 1 tests that the probabilities are finite and equal `softmax(float32(logits))` within the float16-derived bound.
